          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> pet owner, <strong>I can</strong> have the service continuously watch my garden camera feed <strong>so that</strong> I don't have to manually check whether cats are on the sofa.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> M</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>The service samples frames at a configurable interval (default 5s) without crashing or leaking memory.</li><li>Sampling works with both RTSP URLs and USB camera indices.</li><li>A dropped camera connection is retried automatically with exponential back-off.</li><li>The current sampling interval and camera source are visible in logs on startup.</li><li>The loop can be stopped cleanly via SIGTERM without leaving zombie threads.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>The service samples frames at a configurable interval (default 5s) without crashing or leaking memory.</li><li>Sampling works with both RTSP URLs and USB camera indices.</li><li>A dropped camera connection is retried automatically with exponential back-off.</li><li>The current sampling interval and camera source are visible in logs on startup.</li><li>The loop can be stopped cleanly via SIGTERM without leaving zombie threads.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }

[

    "type": "tool_use",
    "id": "toolu_01ND4a2dkuAp4R9yBonJ4EmC",
    "name": "mcp__azure-devops__wit_create_work_item",
    "input": {
      "project": "NeXtgineer Training - Radu Simionescu",
      "workItemType": "User Story",
      "fields": [
        {
          "name": "System.Title",
          "value": "US-2 · Cat Detection with Human Guard"
        },
        {
          "name": "System.Description",
          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> pet owner, <strong>I can</strong> have the service detect cats on the sofa while ignoring frames where a human is present <strong>so that</strong> I only get alerts about actual cat intrusions, not family activity.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> M</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>Detection uses YOLOv8 and correctly identifies COCO class 15 (cat) and class 0 (person).</li><li>A frame with one or more cats and no persons triggers the webhook.</li><li>A frame with cats AND a person present does not trigger the webhook.</li><li>Confidence threshold is configurable via environment variable.</li><li>Detection results (class, confidence, bounding box) are persisted to the database.</li></ol><p><strong>Dependencies:</strong> US-1</p>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>Detection uses YOLOv8 and correctly identifies COCO class 15 (cat) and class 0 (person).</li><li>A frame with one or more cats and no persons triggers the webhook.</li><li>A frame with cats AND a person present does not trigger the webhook.</li><li>Confidence threshold is configurable via environment variable.</li><li>Detection results (class, confidence, bounding box) are persisted to the database.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }

      "project": "NeXtgineer Training - Radu Simionescu",
      "workItemType": "User Story",
      "fields": [
        {
          "name": "System.Title",
          "value": "US-3 · Webhook Alert on Cat Detection"
        },
        {
          "name": "System.Description",
          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> homeowner, <strong>I can</strong> receive an HTTP webhook notification when cats are detected <strong>so that</strong> I can integrate alerts into my home automation or notification system.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> S</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>Webhook URL is configurable via environment variable.</li><li>The POST payload includes timestamp, number of cats detected, and confidence scores.</li><li>A cooldown period prevents repeated alerts for the same cat session (configurable, default 5 min).</li><li>Failed webhook calls are logged with the HTTP status code but do not crash the service.</li><li>Webhook is skipped (not an error) if no URL is configured.</li></ol><p><strong>Dependencies:</strong> US-2</p>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>Webhook URL is configurable via environment variable.</li><li>The POST payload includes timestamp, number of cats detected, and confidence scores.</li><li>A cooldown period prevents repeated alerts for the same cat session (configurable, default 5 min).</li><li>Failed webhook calls are logged with the HTTP status code but do not crash the service.</li><li>Webhook is skipped (not an error) if no URL is configured.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }
]

    "id": "toolu_01YMnxMVYd7Nh12JUXmirszr",
    "name": "mcp__azure-devops__wit_create_work_item",
    "input": {
      "project": "NeXtgineer Training - Radu Simionescu",
      "workItemType": "User Story",
      "fields": [
        {
          "name": "System.Title",
          "value": "US-4 · Detection History Web UI"
        },
        {
          "name": "System.Description",
          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> pet owner, <strong>I can</strong> browse past detection events in a web interface <strong>so that</strong> I can review when cats visited and see the captured frames.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> M</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>A paginated list of detections is shown, newest first, with timestamp and cat count.</li><li>Each detection entry shows the captured frame image.</li><li>The list can be filtered by date range.</li><li>The UI works without a frontend build step (vanilla JS + Jinja2).</li><li>The page loads in under 2 seconds for up to 500 stored detections.</li></ol><p><strong>Dependencies:</strong> US-2</p>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>A paginated list of detections is shown, newest first, with timestamp and cat count.</li><li>Each detection entry shows the captured frame image.</li><li>The list can be filtered by date range.</li><li>The UI works without a frontend build step (vanilla JS + Jinja2).</li><li>The page loads in under 2 seconds for up to 500 stored detections.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }
]

> 4/6/2026, 11:43:03 PM

[
  {
    "type": "tool_use",
    "id": "toolu_016FbSziqyHhaFUXSnXbZwCk",
    "name": "mcp__azure-devops__wit_create_work_item",
    "input": {
      "project": "NeXtgineer Training - Radu Simionescu",
      "workItemType": "User Story",
      "fields": [
        {
          "name": "System.Title",
          "value": "US-5 · Detection Labeling for Fine-Tuning"
        },
        {
          "name": "System.Description",
          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> developer, <strong>I can</strong> label detections as correct or incorrect via the web UI <strong>so that</strong> labeled data can be used to fine-tune the model.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> M</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>Each detection in the UI has Accept / Reject buttons.</li><li>Labels are persisted to the database and associated with the detection record.</li><li>Labeled detections are excluded from future labeling queues.</li><li>A summary count of labeled vs unlabeled detections is shown in the UI.</li></ol><p><strong>Dependencies:</strong> US-4</p>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>Each detection in the UI has Accept / Reject buttons.</li><li>Labels are persisted to the database and associated with the detection record.</li><li>Labeled detections are excluded from future labeling queues.</li><li>A summary count of labeled vs unlabeled detections is shown in the UI.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }
]

### Assistant
> 4/6/2026, 11:43:03 PM

[
  {
    "type": "tool_use",
    "id": "toolu_01DVcW3tWXxpB4emsCQitW7B",
    "name": "mcp__azure-devops__wit_create_work_item",
    "input": {
      "project": "NeXtgineer Training - Radu Simionescu",
      "workItemType": "User Story",
      "fields": [
        {
          "name": "System.Title",
          "value": "US-6 · Model Fine-Tuning Trigger"
        },
        {
          "name": "System.Description",
          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> developer, <strong>I can</strong> trigger a fine-tuning run from the web UI using labeled detections <strong>so that</strong> the model improves over time for my specific camera and cats.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> L</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>A \"Start Fine-tuning\" button is available in the UI, enabled only when at least 20 labeled examples exist.</li><li>Triggering fine-tuning calls the POST /training/fine-tune endpoint.</li><li>The UI shows the current status of a running fine-tuning job (idle / running / done / failed).</li><li>The fine-tuning job runs asynchronously and does not block camera sampling.</li><li>On completion, the new model weights are loaded and used for subsequent detections.</li></ol><p><strong>Dependencies:</strong> US-5</p>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>A \"Start Fine-tuning\" button is available in the UI, enabled only when at least 20 labeled examples exist.</li><li>Triggering fine-tuning calls the POST /training/fine-tune endpoint.</li><li>The UI shows the current status of a running fine-tuning job (idle / running / done / failed).</li><li>The fine-tuning job runs asynchronously and does not block camera sampling.</li><li>On completion, the new model weights are loaded and used for subsequent detections.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }
]

    "name": "mcp__azure-devops__wit_create_work_item",

      "workItemType": "User Story",
      "fields": [
        {
          "name": "System.Title",
          "value": "US-7 · Environment-Based Configuration"
        },
        {
          "name": "System.Description",
          "value": "<p><strong>As a</strong> operator, <strong>I can</strong> configure all service parameters via environment variables or a .env file <strong>so that</strong> I can deploy the service in different environments without code changes.</p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> S</p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong></p><ol><li>All configurable parameters (camera source, detection interval, cooldown, webhook URL, model size, confidence threshold) are documented in .env.example.</li><li>Missing required variables cause a clear error message on startup, not a runtime crash.</li><li>The service runs correctly with only the .env.example defaults (except WEBHOOK_URL which may be blank).</li><li>No secrets or credentials are hardcoded in source files.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        },
        {
          "name": "Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria",
          "value": "<ol><li>All configurable parameters (camera source, detection interval, cooldown, webhook URL, model size, confidence threshold) are documented in .env.example.</li><li>Missing required variables cause a clear error message on startup, not a runtime crash.</li><li>The service runs correctly with only the .env.example defaults (except WEBHOOK_URL which may be blank).</li><li>No secrets or credentials are hardcoded in source files.</li></ol>",
          "format": "Html"
        }
      ]
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }
]

### User

# Implement Story

Plan and implement a user story. **Always creates implementation plan first** for user approval before coding.

## What This Does

**Phase 1: Planning (Plan Mode)**
1. Fetch story via `work-item-sync`
2. Gather context (designs, architecture, tech stack)
3. Consult architect if needed (data models/dependencies/integrations)
4. Create implementation plan with task breakdown
5. Present plan to user for approval

**Phase 2: Implementation (after user approval)**
6. Implement code following the approved plan
7. May create multiple PRs if plan requires it
8. Update story status as work progresses

## Usage

```bash
/z-implement-story 12345
```

Optional context:
```bash
/z-implement-story 12345 Use React with PrimeReact. Focus on accessibility.
```

## Prerequisites

- Work item MCP configured (ADO, Jira, or GitHub) OR manual story input
- Story with clear acceptance criteria
- Git working directory clean

---

## Process

### Phase 1: Planning (Plan Mode)

**Step 1: Gather Context**

Fetch story via `work-item-sync`:
- Read title, description, acceptance criteria
- **Check for Figma designs:**
  - If story has Figma link: Fetch designs via `design-extraction` skill (runs as subagent)
  - If story involves UI/frontend work but no Figma link: Ask user for Figma URL
- Check parent Epic for context
- Validate story is ready (has ACs, not closed, no blockers)

**Parallel reconnaissance (use `codebase-scout` agent):**
Run these in parallel before proceeding to scope analysis:
1. `tech-stack` -- Identify project tech stack from manifest files
2. `architecture-summary` -- Summarize architecture docs and ADRs relevant to this story

Use scout findings as input for Step 2 (scope analysis) and Step 3 (architect consultation decision).

**Step 2: Analyze Scope**

Determine:
- **Story type:** Frontend-Only, Backend-Only, or Full-Stack (CRITICAL for praxis loading optimization)
- Complexity: Simple, Moderate, Complex
- Estimated effort: Hours/days

Pass story type to subagents -- they use it for conditional praxis loading.

**Step 3: Consult Architect If Needed**

Using the scout's architecture-summary from Step 1, determine if architect consultation is required.

**Consult `zdlc:system-architect` ONLY IF decision not already documented:**
- Data model changes (not covered by existing schema design)
- New dependencies (not already approved in architecture docs)
- External API integrations (new integration patterns)
- Performance-critical features (>1000 req/sec, not addressed in docs)
- Security-sensitive data (auth, payments, PII - new patterns)
- Caching or resiliency patterns (not documented)

**Step 4: Create Implementation Plan**

Use Task tool with `subagent_type='Plan'` to create strategic plan with:
- High-level task breakdown (what needs to be done, not how)
- Architectural approach and key decisions
- Components/endpoints to implement (names, not code)
- Testing strategy
- Documentation needs
- Whether to split into multiple PRs (if large)

**Important:** Plan should be strategic (what/why), not tactical (how/code snippets).

**Step 5: Persist Plan & Get Approval**

**Structure:** See [/heuristics/project-structure.md](../heuristics/project-structure.md) for folder structure details.

**Save to:** `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/`

**Files to create:**
- `planning/plan.md` - Implementation plan (strategic)
- `planning/architecture/architect-notes.md` - If architect consulted
- `planning/architecture/adr-*.md` - Feature-specific ADRs (if any)
- `planning/designs/` - Figma exports (if applicable)
- `planning/notes.md` - Additional context

**Present to user:**
1. Save all planning artifacts
2. Message: "Implementation plan saved to stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/plan.md. Please review and edit as needed in your IDE, then reply 'approved' to proceed."
3. WAIT for user to review/edit plan in IDE
4. WAIT for user approval (user replies "approved" or similar)
5. Read final plan from file (user may have modified it)

---

### Phase 2: Implementation (After Approval)

**Read the final approved plan from `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/plan.md`** (user may have edited it).

**During implementation, track progress:**
- Create `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/implementation/progress.md` to log key decisions
- Update as implementation progresses
- Record any deviations from plan with reasoning

**Spawn a FRESH `zdlc:senior-developer` agent** (using Task tool) with ONLY:
- **Story type:** Frontend-Only, Backend-Only, or Full-Stack (for conditional praxis loading)
- Story details
- Acceptance criteria
- **Final approved implementation plan** (read from file)
- **Architecture guidance from planning** (if architect was consulted OR if SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md/ADRs exist)
- Figma designs (if any)

**Instructions to pass to senior-developer:**
- Architecture review completed during planning. Follow documented architecture. Do NOT re-consult architect unless NEW concerns arise.
- Story type (Frontend-Only/Backend-Only/Full-Stack) -- agent handles conditional praxis loading internally.

**Pipeline check (before implementation):**
- Scan for existing CI/CD pipeline (`.azure-pipelines/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`)
- If no pipeline found: Suggest running `/z-pipeline-create` before or after implementation
- Do NOT block implementation — pipeline creation is a suggestion, not a gate

**Implementation approach:**
- Create branch(es) per plan
- Fetch current library docs via context7
- Implement code with tests
- Create PR(s) as work completes
- Update story status via `work-item-sync`

**If plan requires multiple PRs:**
- Implement incrementally
- Create PR for each logical chunk
- Link all PRs to story
- Update story to "In Review" when first PR is ready

---

## Success Criteria

**Planning phase complete when:**
- ✅ Story context gathered
- ✅ Architect consulted (if needed)
- ✅ All planning artifacts saved to `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/`
- ✅ User reviewed and approved plan (possibly with edits)

**Implementation complete when:**
- ✅ All acceptance criteria met
- ✅ Tests passing (>80% coverage)
- ✅ No linting/type errors
- ✅ Accessibility validated (WCAG 2.1 AA)
- ✅ Documentation updated
- ✅ PR(s) created and linked
- ✅ Story status updated appropriately

---

## References

**Agents:**
- `Plan` - Creates implementation plan
- `zdlc:system-architect` - Architectural decisions (during planning)
- `zdlc:senior-developer` - Implementation (after plan approval)
- `zdlc:platform-engineer` - Consulted by system-architect for infrastructure concerns

**Praxis:** Agents follow their own praxis/context files for detailed decision logic.

**Related Commands:**
- `/z-pipeline-create` - Create CI/CD pipeline (suggested if none exists)
- `/z-test-story` - Run testing workflow after implementation

    "content": "1\t\"\"\"\n2\tBackground service: reads frames from the camera stream on an interval,\n3\truns detection, persists results, and fires the webhook when appropriate.\n4\t\"\"\"\n5\t\n6\timport asyncio\n7\timport logging\n8\tfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\n9\t\n10\timport cv2\n11\timport numpy as np\n12\t\n13\tfrom cat_detector.config import settings\n14\tfrom cat_detector.database import SessionLocal\n15\tfrom cat_detector.detector import detect, save_frame, should_trigger_webhook\n16\tfrom cat_detector.models import Detection\n17\tfrom cat_detector.webhook import call_webhook\n18\t\n19\tlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n20\t\n21\t_last_webhook_time: datetime | None = None\n22\t\n23\t\n24\tdef _grab_frame() -> np.ndarray | None:\n25\t    \"\"\"Grab a single frame from the configured camera source.\"\"\"\n26\t    source = settings.camera_url\n27\t    # Allow numeric string (\"0\") as device index\n28\t    try:\n29\t        source = int(source)\n30\t    except ValueError:\n31\t        pass\n32\t\n33\t    cap = cv2.VideoCapture(source)\n34\t    if not cap.isOpened():\n35\t        logger.error(\"Cannot open camera source: %s\", settings.camera_url)\n36\t        return None\n37\t    ret, frame = cap.read()\n38\t    cap.release()\n39\t    return frame if ret else None\n40\t\n41\t\n42\tasync def _process_frame(frame: np.ndarray) -> None:\n43\t    global _last_webhook_time\n44\t\n45\t    result = detect(frame)\n46\t\n47\t    if result.cat_count == 0 and result.human_count == 0:\n48\t        return  # Nothing interesting — don't store\n49\t\n50\t    trigger, skip_reason = should_trigger_webhook(result)\n51\t\n52\t    # Enforce cooldown\n53\t    if trigger and _last_webhook_time is not None:\n54\t        elapsed = datetime.utcnow() - _last_webhook_time\n55\t        if elapsed < timedelta(seconds=settings.webhook_cooldown_seconds):\n56\t            trigger = False\n57\t            skip_reason = \"cooldown\"\n58\t\n59\t    # Persist frame to disk\n60\t    timestamp = datetime.utcnow().strftime(\"%Y%m%d_%H%M%S\")\n61\t    filename = f\"{timestamp}_cats{result.cat_count}_humans{result.human_count}.jpg\"\n62\t    frame_path = save_frame(result.annotated_frame, settings.frames_dir, filename)\n63\t\n64\t    # Persist detection record\n65\t    with SessionLocal() as db:\n66\t        detection = Detection(\n67\t            cat_count=result.cat_count,\n68\t            human_count=result.human_count,\n69\t            max_cat_confidence=result.max_cat_confidence,\n70\t            webhook_triggered=trigger,\n71\t            webhook_skipped_reason=skip_reason,\n72\t            frame_path=str(frame_path),\n73\t        )\n74\t        db.add(detection)\n75\t        db.commit()\n76\t        db.refresh(detection)\n77\t        detection_id = detection.id\n78\t\n79\t    if trigger:\n80\t        success = await call_webhook(detection_id, result.cat_count, result.max_cat_confidence)\n81\t        if success:\n82\t            _last_webhook_time = datetime.utcnow()\n83\t\n84\t\n85\tasync def run_camera_loop() -> None:\n86\t    \"\"\"Main loop — runs indefinitely, sampling a frame every interval.\"\"\"\n87\t    logger.info(\n88\t        \"Camera loop started (source=%s, interval=%ds)\",\n89\t        settings.camera_url,\n90\t        settings.detection_interval_seconds,\n91\t    )\n92\t    while True:\n93\t        try:\n94\t            frame = await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(None, _grab_frame)\n95\t            if frame is not None:\n96\t                await _process_frame(frame)\n97\t        except Exception:\n98\t            logger.exception(\"Unexpected error in camera loop — continuing\")\n99\t\n100\t        await asyncio.sleep(settings.detection_interval_seconds)\n101\t"

Follow the documented approach below. Do NOT re-consult the architect.**\n\n---\n\n## Story\n\nAs a pet owner, I can have the service continuously watch my garden camera feed so that I don't have to manually check whether cats are on the sofa.\n\n**Acceptance Criteria:**\n1. The service samples frames at a configurable interval (default 5s) without crashing or leaking memory.\n2. Sampling works with both RTSP URLs and USB camera indices.\n3. A dropped camera connection is retried automatically with exponential back-off.\n4. The current sampling interval and camera source are visible in logs on startup.\n5. The loop can be stopped cleanly via SIGTERM without leaving zombie threads.\n\n---\n\n## Approved Implementation Plan\n\nMost ACs are already implemented. The only gap is **AC3: exponential backoff**.\n\n### Task 1: Add exponential backoff to `src/cat_detector/camera.py`\n\nCurrent `run_camera_loop()` just logs error and sleeps the normal interval when `_grab_frame()` returns None.\n\nAdd backoff state to the loop:\n- Track `consecutive_failures` counter\n- On failure: delay = min(base_interval * 2^failures, max_delay), log the retry with delay\n- On success: reset `consecutive_failures = 0`, use normal `detection_interval_seconds`\n- `max_delay` comes from new config setting `camera_reconnect_max_delay`\n\n### Task 2: Add `camera_reconnect_max_delay: int = 60` to `src/cat_detector/config.py`\n\n### Task 3: Write `tests/test_camera.py`\n\nTest the backoff logic by mocking `_grab_frame` and `_process_frame`:\n- Consecutive failures increase delay (2x per failure)\n- Cap at max delay\n- Successful grab resets delay\n- Use `unittest.mock.patch` and `pytest-asyncio`\n\n### Task 4: Update `.env.example`\n\nAdd `CAMERA_RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY=60` with a comment.\n\n---\n\n## Key files to read first\n\n- `src/cat_detector/camera.py` — current loop implementation\n- `src/cat_detector/config.py` — settings model\n- `.env.example` — existing env vars\n- `tests/test_detector.py` — existing test patterns to follow\n- `pyproject.toml` — test dependencies\n\n---\n\n## Implementation notes\n\n- Create a branch `feat/6104-live-camera-monitoring` from main\n- Do NOT create a PR — just implement the code and run the tests\n- Do NOT modify any files outside of the scope above\n- Tests must pass with `pytest tests/` (no real camera needed — mock everything)\n- The project uses `pytest-asyncio` for async tests"
    },

### Assistant
> 4/11/2026, 3:16:06 PM

## Backlog Review

---

### 1. Stories That Should Be Split

**#6109 — US-6 · Model Fine-Tuning Trigger** (currently L)

This story mixes three distinct concerns that should be separate:

- **US-6a: Fine-tuning API endpoint** — Backend: implement `_run_finetuning`, export labeled data to YOLO format, run training, persist new weights. This is the hard part.
- **US-6b: Hot-reload model weights** — Backend: swap the running detector's model after fine-tuning without restarting the service. Has its own concurrency risks.
- **US-6c: Fine-tuning UI controls** — Frontend: button with 20-label gate, status polling (idle/running/done/failed). Straightforward once the API exists.

Splitting makes each piece independently testable and reviewable.

---

### 2. Unclear Acceptance Criteria

**#6107 — US-4 · Detection History Web UI**
- AC3 "filtered by date range" — no detail on the UI control (date pickers? URL params? presets like "today/last 7 days"?). Should clarify the interaction model.
- AC5 "loads in under 2 seconds for 500 detections" — what conditions? Cold start? With images loaded? This needs a clearer measurement definition or should be dropped in favor of "pagination limits results to N per page."

**#6108 — US-5 · Detection Labeling for Fine-Tuning**
- AC3 "excluded from future labeling queues" — there is no concept of a "labeling queue" elsewhere in the backlog. Does this mean a filtered view? A separate page? Needs clarification.
- Missing AC: no mention of undo/relabel. Once labeled, can a user change their mind?

**#6109 — US-6 · Model Fine-Tuning Trigger**
- AC5 "new model weights are loaded and used" — no criteria for what happens if fine-tuning produces a *worse* model. No rollback mechanism mentioned.
- AC1 "at least 20 labeled examples" — is this 20 total, or 20 of each class (accept/reject)? Imbalanced labels would produce a bad model.

---

### 3. Priority Adjustments (Based on Dependencies)

Current state: all stories are Priority 2. Recommended ordering:

| Priority | Story | Rationale |
|----------|-------|-----------|
| **1 (Must)** | #6110 — US-7 · Configuration | Foundation — every other story depends on correct config. Should be done first (or validated as already done). |
| **1 (Must)** | #6104 — US-1 · Camera Monitoring | Core loop — nothing works without it. *Already implemented.* |
| **1 (Must)** | #6105 — US-2 · Cat Detection | Core value prop — depends on US-1, blocks US-3 and US-4. |
| **2 (Should)** | #6106 — US-3 · Webhook Alert | Depends on US-2. Primary user-facing output. |
| **2 (Should)** | #6107 — US-4 · Detection History UI | Depends on US-2. Needed for labeling (US-5). |
| **3 (Could)** | #6108 — US-5 · Labeling | Depends on US-4. Nice-to-have for MVP. |
| **4 (Won't yet)** | #6109 — US-6 · Fine-Tuning | Depends on US-5. Should split first. Defer until labeling is proven. |

---

### 4. Complexity Estimates

| Story | Original | Revised | Notes |
|-------|----------|---------|-------|
| #6104 — US-1 Camera Loop | M | **S** | Already implemented. Backoff was the only gap. |
| #6105 — US-2 Detection | M | **M** | YOLO inference + trigger logic + DB persistence. Straightforward but multi-layered. |
| #6106 — US-3 Webhook | S | **S** | Agree. Simple httpx POST + cooldown. |
| #6107 — US-4 History UI | M | **M** | Pagination + date filtering + image serving. Standard full-stack work. |
| #6108 — US-5 Labeling | M | **S** | Two buttons + one DB field + a count query. Simpler than it looks. |
| #6109 — US-6 Fine-Tuning | L | **XL** (unsplit) / **M+S+S** (split) | YOLO fine-tuning pipeline + hot-reload + UI. Too much for one story. |
| #6110 — US-7 Configuration | S | **S** | Mostly already done via pydantic-settings. Validation + .env.example audit. |

---

Want me to update the stories in ADO with these findings (priorities, tags, comments)?

\"System.Description\": \"<p><strong>As a</strong> pet owner, <strong>I can</strong> have the service detect cats on the sofa while ignoring frames where a human is present <strong>so that</strong> I only get alerts about actual cat intrusions, not family activity. </p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> M </p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong> </p><ol><li>Detection uses YOLOv8 and correctly identifies COCO class 15 (cat) and class 0 (person). </li><li>A frame with one or more cats and no persons triggers the webhook. </li><li>A frame with cats AND a person present does not trigger the webhook. </li><li>Confidence threshold is configurable via environment variable. </li><li>Detection results (class, confidence, bounding box) are persisted to the database. </li> \"System.Description\": \"<p><strong>As a</strong> homeowner, <strong>I can</strong> receive an HTTP webhook notification when cats are detected <strong>so that</strong> I can integrate alerts into my home automation or notification system. </p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> S </p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong> </p><ol><li>Webhook URL is configurable via environment variable. </li><li>The POST payload includes timestamp, number of cats detected, and confidence scores. </li><li>A cooldown period prevents repeated alerts for the same cat session (configurable, default 5 min). </li><li>Failed webhook calls are logged with the HTTP status code but do not crash the service. </li><li>Webhook is skipped (not an error) if no URL is configured. </li> \"<p><strong>As a</strong> developer, <strong>I can</strong> trigger a fine-tuning run from the web UI using labeled detections <strong>so that</strong> the model improves over time for my specific camera and cats. </p><p><strong>Complexity:</strong> L </p><p><strong>Acceptance Criteria:</strong> </p><ol><li>A &quot;Start Fine-tuning&quot; button is available in the UI, enabled only when at least 20 labeled examples exist. </li><li>Triggering fine-tuning calls the POST /training/fine-tune endpoint. </li><li>The UI shows the current status of a running fine-tuning job (idle / running / done / failed). </li><li>The fine-tuning job runs asynchronously and does not block camera sampling. </li><li>On completion, the new model weights are loaded and used for subsequent detections. </li> </ol><p><strong>Dependencies:</strong> US-5 </p>\",\n    \"Microsoft.VSTS.Common.AcceptanceCriteria\": \"<ol><li>A &quot;Start Fine-tuning&quot; button is available in the UI, enabled only when at [
  {

      "description": "Create PRD for Cat Detector",

      "prompt": "Create a comprehensive PRD (Product Requirements Document) for the **Cat Detector** system. Save it to `/Users/radu.simionescu/work/claude training/NeXtgineer Training - Radu Simionescu/cat-detector/docs/prd.md`.\n\n## Context\n\nThis is a garden surveillance system that watches an RTSP/USB camera stream pointed at an outdoor garden sofa. When cats are detected (and no humans are present), it fires a configurable webhook. It also exposes a web UI for reviewing past detections, labeling them, and triggering model fine-tuning.\n\n## Tech Stack\n- FastAPI, YOLOv8 (ultralytics), OpenCV, SQLAlchemy + SQLite, Jinja2 templates, httpx, Pydantic Settings\n\n## Existing User Stories in ADO (backlog)\n\n1. **US-1 · Live Camera Monitoring** (P1, S) — Service samples frames at configurable interval (5s default), supports RTSP/USB, exponential backoff on dropped connections, clean SIGTERM shutdown. *Already implemented.*\n\n2. **US-2 · Cat Detection with Human Guard** (P1, M) — YOLOv8 detects cats (class 15) and humans (class 0). Cats-only triggers webhook; cats+humans suppressed. Configurable confidence threshold. Results persisted to DB.\n\n3. **US-3 · Webhook Alert on Cat Detection** (P2, S) — HTTP POST webhook with cooldown (default 5 min). Payload includes timestamp, cat count, confidence scores. Graceful failure handling.\n\n4. **US-4 · Detection History Web UI** (P2, M) — Paginated list of detections (newest first), frame images, date range filtering. Vanilla JS + Jinja2. *Has open AC clarification questions about date filter UI and performance metric.*\n\n5. **US-5 · Detection Labeling for Fine-Tuning** (P3, S) — Accept/Reject buttons per detection, labels persisted to DB, labeled/unlabeled counts. *Has open questions about undo/relabel and \"labeling queue\" concept.*\n\n6. **US-6 · Model Fine-Tuning Trigger** (P4, XL unsplit) — Fine-tuning button (min 20 labels), async training job, status polling, hot-reload new weights. *Flagged for splitting into 3 stories: API endpoint (M), hot-reload (S), UI controls (S). Open questions about rollback and label balance.*\n\n7. **US-7 · Environment-Based Configuration** (P1, S) — All params via .env, clear startup errors, no hardcoded secrets.\n\n## PRD Requirements\n\nThe PRD must include these sections:\n\n### 1. Problem Statement\nWhy does this product exist? What pain point does it solve for the user?\n\n### 2. User Personas Affected\nDefine 2-3 personas with their goals, pain points, and how they interact with the system. Consider: pet owner (primary), developer/operator (secondary), possibly home automation enthusiast.\n\n### 3. Success Metrics\nMeasurable KPIs that indicate the product is working. Include both technical metrics (detection accuracy, uptime, latency) and user-facing metrics (time to detect, false positive rate, user engagement with labeling).\n\n### 4. Proposed Solution Overview\nHigh-level architecture and feature set. Reference the existing stories. Explain the detection pipeline, webhook integration, web UI, and fine-tuning loop. Include a system flow diagram if possible.\n\n### 5. Out of Scope Items\nExplicitly list what this product does NOT do (e.g., multi-camera support, cloud deployment, mobile app, real-time video streaming, species-specific cat identification, audio alerts, etc.)\n\n### 6. Dependencies & Risks\nWhat could go wrong? What external factors matter?\n\n### 7. Release Strategy\nMVP scope vs. future phases, mapped to story priorities.\n\nFormat as a well-structured markdown document suitable for stakeholder review."
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"

### User

# Implement Story

Plan and implement a user story. **Always creates implementation plan first** for user approval before coding.

## What This Does

**Phase 1: Planning (Plan Mode)**
1. Fetch story via `work-item-sync`
2. Gather context (designs, architecture, tech stack)
3. Consult architect if needed (data models/dependencies/integrations)
4. Create implementation plan with task breakdown
5. Present plan to user for approval

**Phase 2: Implementation (after user approval)**
6. Implement code following the approved plan
7. May create multiple PRs if plan requires it
8. Update story status as work progresses

## Usage

```bash
/z-implement-story 12345
```

Optional context:
```bash
/z-implement-story 12345 Use React with PrimeReact. Focus on accessibility.
```

## Prerequisites

- Work item MCP configured (ADO, Jira, or GitHub) OR manual story input
- Story with clear acceptance criteria
- Git working directory clean

---

## Process

### Phase 1: Planning (Plan Mode)

**Step 1: Gather Context**

Fetch story via `work-item-sync`:
- Read title, description, acceptance criteria
- **Check for Figma designs:**
  - If story has Figma link: Fetch designs via `design-extraction` skill (runs as subagent)
  - If story involves UI/frontend work but no Figma link: Ask user for Figma URL
- Check parent Epic for context
- Validate story is ready (has ACs, not closed, no blockers)

**Parallel reconnaissance (use `codebase-scout` agent):**
Run these in parallel before proceeding to scope analysis:
1. `tech-stack` -- Identify project tech stack from manifest files
2. `architecture-summary` -- Summarize architecture docs and ADRs relevant to this story

Use scout findings as input for Step 2 (scope analysis) and Step 3 (architect consultation decision).

**Step 2: Analyze Scope**

Determine:
- **Story type:** Frontend-Only, Backend-Only, or Full-Stack (CRITICAL for praxis loading optimization)
- Complexity: Simple, Moderate, Complex
- Estimated effort: Hours/days

Pass story type to subagents -- they use it for conditional praxis loading.

**Step 3: Consult Architect If Needed**

Using the scout's architecture-summary from Step 1, determine if architect consultation is required.

**Consult `zdlc:system-architect` ONLY IF decision not already documented:**
- Data model changes (not covered by existing schema design)
- New dependencies (not already approved in architecture docs)
- External API integrations (new integration patterns)
- Performance-critical features (>1000 req/sec, not addressed in docs)
- Security-sensitive data (auth, payments, PII - new patterns)
- Caching or resiliency patterns (not documented)

**Step 4: Create Implementation Plan**

Use Task tool with `subagent_type='Plan'` to create strategic plan with:
- High-level task breakdown (what needs to be done, not how)
- Architectural approach and key decisions
- Components/endpoints to implement (names, not code)
- Testing strategy
- Documentation needs
- Whether to split into multiple PRs (if large)

**Important:** Plan should be strategic (what/why), not tactical (how/code snippets).

**Step 5: Persist Plan & Get Approval**

**Structure:** See [/heuristics/project-structure.md](../heuristics/project-structure.md) for folder structure details.

**Save to:** `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/`

**Files to create:**
- `planning/plan.md` - Implementation plan (strategic)
- `planning/architecture/architect-notes.md` - If architect consulted
- `planning/architecture/adr-*.md` - Feature-specific ADRs (if any)
- `planning/designs/` - Figma exports (if applicable)
- `planning/notes.md` - Additional context

**Present to user:**
1. Save all planning artifacts
2. Message: "Implementation plan saved to stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/plan.md. Please review and edit as needed in your IDE, then reply 'approved' to proceed."
3. WAIT for user to review/edit plan in IDE
4. WAIT for user approval (user replies "approved" or similar)
5. Read final plan from file (user may have modified it)

---

### Phase 2: Implementation (After Approval)

**Read the final approved plan from `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/plan.md`** (user may have edited it).

**During implementation, track progress:**
- Create `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/implementation/progress.md` to log key decisions
- Update as implementation progresses
- Record any deviations from plan with reasoning

**Spawn a FRESH `zdlc:senior-developer` agent** (using Task tool) with ONLY:
- **Story type:** Frontend-Only, Backend-Only, or Full-Stack (for conditional praxis loading)
- Story details
- Acceptance criteria
- **Final approved implementation plan** (read from file)
- **Architecture guidance from planning** (if architect was consulted OR if SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md/ADRs exist)
- Figma designs (if any)

**Instructions to pass to senior-developer:**
- Architecture review completed during planning. Follow documented architecture. Do NOT re-consult architect unless NEW concerns arise.
- Story type (Frontend-Only/Backend-Only/Full-Stack) -- agent handles conditional praxis loading internally.

**Pipeline check (before implementation):**
- Scan for existing CI/CD pipeline (`.azure-pipelines/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`)
- If no pipeline found: Suggest running `/z-pipeline-create` before or after implementation
- Do NOT block implementation — pipeline creation is a suggestion, not a gate

**Implementation approach:**
- Create branch(es) per plan
- Fetch current library docs via context7
- Implement code with tests
- Create PR(s) as work completes
- Update story status via `work-item-sync`

**If plan requires multiple PRs:**
- Implement incrementally
- Create PR for each logical chunk
- Link all PRs to story
- Update story to "In Review" when first PR is ready

---

## Success Criteria

**Planning phase complete when:**
- ✅ Story context gathered
- ✅ Architect consulted (if needed)
- ✅ All planning artifacts saved to `stories/[story-id]-[slug]/planning/`
- ✅ User reviewed and approved plan (possibly with edits)

**Implementation complete when:**
- ✅ All acceptance criteria met
- ✅ Tests passing (>80% coverage)
- ✅ No linting/type errors
- ✅ Accessibility validated (WCAG 2.1 AA)
- ✅ Documentation updated
- ✅ PR(s) created and linked
- ✅ Story status updated appropriately

---

## References

**Agents:**
- `Plan` - Creates implementation plan
- `zdlc:system-architect` - Architectural decisions (during planning)
- `zdlc:senior-developer` - Implementation (after plan approval)
- `zdlc:platform-engineer` - Consulted by system-architect for infrastructure concerns

**Praxis:** Agents follow their own praxis/context files for detailed decision logic.

**Related Commands:**
- `/z-pipeline-create` - Create CI/CD pipeline (suggested if none exists)
- `/z-test-story` - Run testing workflow after implementation

      "content": "# Implementation Plan — US-7 · Environment-Based Configuration (#6110)\n\n## Story\n\n**As a** operator, **I can** configure all service parameters via environment variables or a .env file **so that** I can deploy the service in different environments without code changes.\n\n## Current State\n\nPydantic-settings integration is already in place (`config.py`). All 12 settings have defaults and are documented in `.env.example`. The remaining work is validation hardening and startup error UX.\n\n## Tasks\n\n### Task 1: Add pydantic field validators to `config.py`\n\nAdd `field_validator` checks for:\n- `detection_confidence`: must be between 0.0 and 1.0\n- `port`: must be between 1 and 65535\n- `detection_interval_seconds`: must be > 0\n- `webhook_cooldown_seconds`: must be >= 0\n- `camera_reconnect_max_delay`: must be > 0\n\nThese raise `ValidationError` with clear messages if .env has invalid values.\n\n### Task 2: Add startup validation wrapper in `main.py`\n\nWrap the `Settings()` instantiation so that if pydantic raises `ValidationError`, the service prints a human-friendly error message listing each invalid field and exits with code 1 — instead of a raw traceback.\n\nCurrently `settings = Settings()` is at module level in `config.py`. Move the error handling to the lifespan or add a `try/except` in config.py that catches `ValidationError` and re-raises with a clean message.\n\n### Task 3: Fix `.env.example` defaults alignment\n\n- Change `CAMERA_URL` to use a comment showing the RTSP example but default to `0` (matches config.py default)\n- Ensure all values in `.env.example` match `config.py` defaults so copying the file works out of the box\n\n### Task 4: Add unit tests (`tests/test_config.py`)\n\n- Valid defaults load without error\n- Invalid confidence (e.g., 1.5) raises clear validation error\n- Invalid port (e.g., 99999) raises clear validation error\n- Negative interval raises clear validation error\n- Environment variables override defaults correctly\n\n## Approach\n\n- Single PR, small scope\n- No data model changes, no new dependencies\n- Backend-only\n\n## Testing Strategy\n\n- Unit tests for config validation (mock env vars)\n- Verify existing tests still pass\n"
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }
  }
]

All configurable parameters (camera source, detection interval, cooldown, webhook URL, model size, confidence threshold) are documented in .env.example.\n2. Missing required variables cause a clear error message on startup, not a runtime crash.\n3. The service runs correctly with only the .env.example defaults (except WEBHOOK_URL which may be blank).\n4. No secrets or credentials are hardcoded in source files.\n\n---\n\n## Approved Implementation Plan\n\n### Task 1: Add pydantic field validators to `src/cat_detector/config.py`\n\nAdd `field_validator` checks for:\n- `detection_confidence`: must be between 0.0 and 1.0\n- `port`: must be between 1 and 65535\n- `detection_interval_seconds`: must be > 0\n- `webhook_cooldown_seconds`: must be >= 0\n- `camera_reconnect_max_delay`: must be > 0\n\nThese raise `ValidationError` with clear messages if .env has invalid values.\n\nCurrent config.py content:\n```python\nfrom pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict\n\n\nclass Settings(BaseSettings):\n    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(env_file=\".env\", env_file_encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    # Camera\n    camera_url: str = \"0\"\n    camera_reconnect_max_delay: int = 60\n\n    # Detection\n    detection_model: str = \"yolov8n.pt\"\n    detection_confidence: float = 0.5\n    detection_interval_seconds: int = 5\n    webhook_cooldown_seconds: int = 300\n\n    # Webhook\n    webhook_url: str = \"\"\n    webhook_secret: str = \"\"\n\n    # Storage\n    database_url: str = \"sqlite:///./cat_detector.db\"\n    frames_dir: str = \"./data/frames\"\n\n    # Web UI\n    host: str = \"0.0.0.0\"\n    port: int = 8000\n\n\nsettings = Settings()\n```\n\n### Task 2: Add startup validation wrapper in `src/cat_detector/config.py`\n\nWrap `Settings()` instantiation so that if pydantic raises `ValidationError`, the module prints a human-friendly error message listing each invalid field and exits with code 1 — instead of a raw traceback.\n\nAdd a `try/except` around `settings = Settings()` that catches `ValidationError`, prints friendly errors, and calls `sys.exit(1)`.\n\n### Task 3: Fix `.env.example` defaults alignment\n\nChange `CAMERA_URL` default to `0` (matches config.py) with the RTSP example shown only as a comment. Ensure all values match config.py defaults so copying the file works out of the box.\n\nCurrent .env.example:\n```\n# Camera stream (RTSP, HTTP, or local device index)\nCAMERA_URL=rtsp://user:pass@192.168.1.100:554/stream\n# Use 0 for webcam, or a path to a video file for local testing\n# CAMERA_URL=0\n\n# Camera reconnect settings\nCAMERA_RECONNECT_MAX_DELAY=60       # Maximum backoff delay (seconds) when camera connection is lost\n\n# Detection settings\nDETECTION_MODEL=yolov8n.pt          # yolov8n/s/m/l/x — trade speed for accuracy\nDETECTION_CONFIDENCE=0.5            # Minimum confidence threshold (0.0–1.0)\nDETECTION_INTERVAL_SECONDS=5        # How often to sample a frame from the stream\nWEBHOOK_COOLDOWN_SECONDS=300        # Minimum time between consecutive webhook calls\n\n# Webhook\nWEBHOOK_URL=https://your-webhook-endpoint.example.com/cat-alert\nWEBHOOK_SECRET=                     # Optional: added as X-Webhook-Secret header\n\n# Storage\nDATABASE_URL=sqlite:///./cat_detector.db\nFRAMES_DIR=./data/frames            # Where detection frames are saved\n\n# Web UI\nHOST=0.0.0.0\nPORT=8000\n```\n\n### Task 4: Write `tests/test_config.py`\n\nTest the validation logic:\n- Valid defaults load without error\n- Invalid confidence (e.g., 1.5) raises validation error\n- Invalid port (e.g., 99999) raises validation error\n- Negative interval raises validation error\n- Environment variables override defaults correctly\n\nUse `monkeypatch` or environment variable overrides to test without needing a real .env file. Instantiate `Settings` directly rather than importing the module-level `settings` singleton.\n\n---\n\n## Key files\n\n- `src/cat_detector/config.py` — main changes\n- `.env.example` — alignment fix\n- `tests/test_config.py` — new test file\n- `tests/test_detector.py` — existing tests, reference for patterns\n\n## Implementation notes\n\n- Create branch `feat/6110-environment-config` from main\n- Do NOT create a PR — just implement the code and run tests\n- Tests must pass with `pytest tests/test_config.py`\n- Do NOT add new dependencies — pydantic's `field_validator` is already available via pydantic-settings"
    },
    "caller": {
      "type": "direct"
    }

# Pipeline Create

Generate a CI/CD pipeline configuration based on project context, tech stack, and deployment targets.

## What This Does

1. **Detects project context** — tech stack, existing pipelines, repo structure
2. **Detects CI/CD platform** via `pipeline-sync` skill
3. **Generates pipeline YAML** with all mandatory quality gates
4. **Creates pipeline templates** for reusable stages
5. **Configures deployment stages** with manual approval gates for staging/production
6. **Creates IaC pipeline** if Terraform files exist (separate pipeline)

## Usage

```bash
/z-pipeline-create
/z-pipeline-create Add DAST scanning with ZAP
```

## Prerequisites

- Project with at least a `package.json`, `*.csproj`, `pom.xml`, or similar
- CI/CD platform configured (or will be detected/asked)

## Workflow

You are orchestrating pipeline creation. Invoke `platform-engineer` agent.

### Step 0: Context Gathering

1. **Run `codebase-scout` agent (parallel):**
   - Detect tech stack (languages, frameworks, package managers)
   - Find existing pipeline files
   - Find existing Terraform/IaC files
   - Identify repo structure (monorepo vs single service)

2. **Detect CI/CD platform** via `pipeline-sync` skill

### Step 1: Planning

Invoke `platform-engineer` agent with:
- Tech stack detected
- CI/CD platform
- Repo structure
- Existing pipelines (if any)
- User's additional context ()

**Follow praxis:** `/praxis/devops/pipeline-creation.md`

**Platform engineer creates pipeline plan:**
1. Pipeline structure (stages, jobs, steps)
2. Quality gates (tests, lint, SAST, license scan, coverage, dependency scan, container build)
3. Deployment stages (feature → dev → staging → production)
4. Manual approval gates for staging and production
5. Pipeline templates for reusability
6. IaC pipeline if Terraform exists

### Step 2: Quality Gate — User Approval

Present pipeline plan to user:
- Pipeline stages and structure
- Quality gates included
- Deployment strategy per environment
- Template structure

Ask: "Approve this pipeline plan? (yes/no)"
Wait for explicit confirmation.

### Step 3: Implementation

After approval, `platform-engineer` generates:
- Main pipeline YAML
- Template files for reusable stages
- IaC pipeline (if applicable)
- Service connections / variable group references
- Documentation (README section or inline comments)

### Step 4: Validation

- Verify YAML syntax is valid
- Verify all mandatory quality gates are present
- Verify manual approval gates for staging and production
- Verify secrets are referenced (not hardcoded)

## Notes

- Suggested by `/z-implement-story` if no pipeline exists
- IaC pipeline is separate from application pipeline
- Manual approval gates are mandatory for staging (1+ approver) and production (2+ approvers)
- Approvers cannot be the pipeline triggerer

## Related Commands

- `/z-pipeline-deploy` - Trigger deployment through pipeline
- `/z-infra-provision` - Provision infrastructure
- `/z-infra-environments` - Set up environment configs

main\n\nvariables:\n  pythonVersion: \"3.11\"\n\nstages:\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  # Stage 1: Lint\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  - stage: Lint\n    displayName: \"Lint\"\n    jobs:\n      - job: Ruff\n        displayName: \"Ruff lint check\"\n        pool:\n          vmImage: ubuntu-latest\n        steps:\n          - task: UsePythonVersion@0\n            inputs:\n              versionSpec: $(pythonVersion)\n            displayName: \"Use Python $(pythonVersion)\"\n\n          - script: pip install ruff\n            displayName: \"Install ruff\"\n\n          - script: ruff check src/ tests/\n            displayName: \"Run ruff\"\n\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  # Stage 2: Test\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  - stage: Test\n    displayName: \"Test\"\n    dependsOn: Lint\n    jobs:\n      - job: Pytest\n        displayName: \"Unit tests\"\n        pool:\n          vmImage: ubuntu-latest\n        steps:\n          - task: UsePythonVersion@0\n            inputs:\n              versionSpec: $(pythonVersion)\n            displayName: \"Use Python $(pythonVersion)\"\n\n          - script: pip install -e \".[dev]\" pytest-cov\n            displayName: \"Install dependencies\"\n\n          - script: >\n              pytest tests/\n              --junitxml=test-results.xml\n              --cov=cat_detector\n              --cov-report=xml:coverage.xml\n              --cov-report=term\n            displayName: \"Run tests with coverage\"\n\n          - task: PublishTestResults@2\n            condition: succeededOrFailed()\n            inputs:\n              testResultsFormat: JUnit\n              testResultsFiles: test-results.xml\n              testRunTitle: \"Cat Detector — Unit Tests\"\n            displayName: \"Publish test results\"\n\n          - task: PublishCodeCoverageResults@2\n            condition: succeededOrFailed()\n            inputs:\n              summaryFileLocation: coverage.xml\n            displayName: \"Publish coverage report\"\n\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  # Stage 3: Build\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  - stage: Build\n    displayName: \"Build\"\n    dependsOn: Test\n    jobs:\n      - job: BuildWheel\n        displayName: \"Build wheel artifact\"\n        pool:\n          vmImage: ubuntu-latest\n        steps:\n          - task: UsePythonVersion@0\n            inputs:\n              versionSpec: $(pythonVersion)\n            displayName: \"Use Python $(pythonVersion)\"\n\n          - script: pip install build\n            displayName: \"Install build tools\"\n\n          - script: python -m build\n            displayName: \"Build wheel\"\n\n          # Verify the package is importable\n          - script: |\n              pip install dist/*.whl\n              python -c \"from cat_detector.main import app; print('Build verification OK')\"\n            displayName: \"Verify build\"\n\n          - task: PublishPipelineArtifact@1\n            inputs:\n              targetPath: dist/\n              artifactName: wheel\n            displayName: \"Publish wheel artifact\"\n\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  # Stage 4: Deploy to Staging (main branch only)\n  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n  -
